I end up skipping all of that just to stay at the workshop, but I don't even enjoy myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and has it been a gradual change or did it start suddenly after something specific happened?\n\nPatient: This heavy, stuck feeling has been hanging over me for a little over two years now, starting around when the construction market slowed down and I opened my furniture studio. It wasn't sudden, but more of a gradual grind as the constant financial strain and pressure of running the business slowly wore me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you felt better for a while, or has it been a consistent low mood for the entire two years?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any breaks or periods where I felt better. It’s been a consistent, heavy feeling pretty much nonstop for the whole two years since I opened the studio. I just haven't been able to shake it at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain, or changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past two years?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any major changes in my weight or my appetite. As for sleep, I just feel tired and worn out all the time, no matter how much sleep I actually get.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling this way for about two years, can you tell me if there have been any times within those two years where your low mood significantly worsened or improved, even if briefly, or has it remained relatively consistent?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, it has stayed really consistent, just a steady and gray grind day after day. There haven't been any times where the mood lifted or I felt genuinely better, even for a few days. It's just been this constant, heavy weight.

It's more of just a constant, heavy feeling of being completely exhausted, hopeless, and stuck.\n\nDoctor: You've had this consistent low mood for about two years without any significant breaks, but can you tell me if there have been any periods where you felt slightly better or had some energy, even if it was just for a short while, or has it been a completely flat mood the entire time?"

There hasn't been a single day of relief.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant anxiety, worry, or fear that something bad might happen, especially related to your business or personal life, during these two years?\n\nPatient: I don't really experience intense anxiety, panic, or a fear that something bad is going to happen. It's more of just a constant, heavy feeling of being completely exhausted, hopeless, and stuck.\n\nDoctor: You've had this consistent low mood for about two years without any significant breaks, but can you tell me if there have been any periods where you felt slightly better or had some energy, even if it was just for a short while, or has it been a completely flat mood the entire time?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any bursts of energy or lighter days at all. It's just been a completely flat, heavy mood day after day since I started the workshop. I've felt exhausted and dragged down the entire time without any real break.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent low mood for over two years without significant improvement, which is helpful in distinguishing between different types of depressive disorders. Can you clarify if you've experienced any depressive episodes that lasted for less than two years at any point in your life before this current period?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever experiencing any periods of depression or low mood like this before this current stretch started two years ago. Before opening the studio, I generally felt okay and didn't have to deal with this kind of heavy feeling."

It wasn't sudden, but more of a gradual grind as the constant financial strain and pressure of running the business slowly wore me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you felt better for a while, or has it been a consistent low mood for the entire two years?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any breaks or periods where I felt better. It’s been a consistent, heavy feeling pretty much nonstop for the whole two years since I opened the studio. I just haven't been able to shake it at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain, or changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past two years?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any major changes in my weight or my appetite. As for sleep, I just feel tired and worn out all the time, no matter how much sleep I actually get.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling this way for about two years, can you tell me if there have been any times within those two years where your low mood significantly worsened or improved, even if briefly, or has it remained relatively consistent?\n\nPatient: No, it has stayed really consistent, just a steady and gray grind day after day. There haven't been any times where the mood lifted or I felt genuinely better, even for a few days. It's just been this constant, heavy weight.\n\nDoctor: You've described a consistent low mood for about two years, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief ones, where you felt nearly back to normal or if the low mood has been unremitting the entire time?\n\nPatient: It has been completely unremitting, with absolutely no times where I felt back to normal. Ever since I opened the studio over two years ago, this heavy feeling has just been there nonstop. There hasn't been a single day of relief.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant anxiety, worry, or fear that something bad might happen, especially related to your business or personal life, during these two years?\n\nPatient: I don't really experience intense anxiety, panic, or a fear that something bad is going to happen. It's more of just a constant, heavy feeling of being completely exhausted, hopeless, and stuck.\n\nDoctor: You've had this consistent low mood for about two years without any significant breaks, but can you tell me if there have been any periods where you felt slightly better or had some energy, even if it was just for a short while, or has it been a completely flat mood the entire time?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any bursts of energy or lighter days at all. It's just been a completely flat, heavy mood day after day since I started the workshop. I've felt exhausted and dragged down the entire time without any real break.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent low mood for over two years without significant improvement, which is helpful in distinguishing between different types of depressive disorders. Can you clarify if you've experienced any depressive episodes that lasted for less than two years at any point in your life before this current period?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever experiencing any periods of depression or low mood like this before this current stretch started two years ago. Before opening the studio, I generally felt okay and didn't have to deal with this kind of heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this is your first experience with such a prolonged low mood, but can you tell me if you've had any previous episodes of feeling down or depressed that lasted for a shorter period, even if it was just a few weeks or months, before the two-year stretch you're currently in?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having any distinct periods of depression or low mood like this before this current two-year stretch started. Before I opened the workshop, my mood was generally stable and I didn't experience anything like this."

Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
